It is a leafy vegetable belonging to the Chicory family, characterized by its red or purple leaves and slightly bitter flavor. It is widely used in roasted, grilled, or raw forms in Italian cuisines to bring about a nice contrast in taste and color to salads and other popular dishes. In recent times, it has gained popularity in British cookery as well. \n\nDepending on the variety in shape, color, and taste, radicchio can have different names, such as:\n\nChioggia Radicchio: Due to its compact, round head and deep burgundy leaves, this variety is also called \"round radicchio\".\n\nTreviso Radicchio: It comes in two forms—the early-harvested Precoce variety with looser leaves and a milder flavor, and the late-harvested Tardivo variety with tighter leaves and a more intense taste.\n\nRosa di Gorizia: With its bright red leaves this variety looks like rosebuds.\n\nVariegato di Castelfranco: This variety was created by crossing radicchio di Treviso and escarole.
